We don't have context of how export is used.\n\nBut we have test_export.py that expects export_csv to support totals. Let's look at test_export.py again. The test expects \"TOTAL,4,6\". That suggests that the total row should have \"TOTAL\" in the first column? Or maybe the test is wrong. Let's examine column_totals: it returns [sum(r[i] for r in rows) for i in range(len(rows[0]))]. For rows [[1,2],[3,4]], total_row = [4,6]. So render_rows([total_row]) would produce \"4,6\". But the test expects \"TOTAL,4,6\". That is, the total row starts with \"TOTAL\" then the totals? That seems like they want a label \"TOTAL\" in the first cell, and then the column totals in subsequent cells. But that would be weird because the row length would be len(total_row)+1? Actually \"TOTAL,4,6\" has three columns: \"TOTAL\", 4, 6. But original rows have 2 columns. So adding a column? Or maybe they want the total row to have the same number of columns, with the first cell being \"TOTAL\" and the rest being the totals? That would be [\"TOTAL\", 4, 6] but that's 3 columns. Original columns are 2. So maybe they want to prepend \"TOTAL\" as a new first column? That would change the structure.\n\nAlternatively, maybe the test is incorrect or outdated. Let's look at test_render_marks_last_row_as_total: it expects render_rows([[1, 2], [3, 4]], mark_last=True) == [\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]. Here they are marking the last row as total by changing the first cell to \"TOTAL\", but keeping the original values? That seems also odd.\n\nPerhaps the intended behavior is different. Let's think about typical export with totals: You have data rows, then a total row at the bottom with sums for each column. That's what test_export_appends_totals_row expects: \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\". Wait, that's \"TOTAL,4,6\". That's three columns: TOTAL, 4, 6. But the data rows are two columns: 1,2 and 3,4. So the total row has an extra column \"TOTAL\" at the front. That suggests the total row is formatted differently: it has a label \"TOTAL\" in the first column, and then the summed values in the subsequent columns. But then the column count mismatches: data rows have 2 columns, total row has 3 columns. That seems inconsistent for a CSV.\n\nMaybe the data rows include a dummy first column? Let's examine the data: [[1, 2], [3, 4]]. If we render these with render_rows, we get \"1,2\" and \"3,4\". That's two columns. The total row \"TOTAL,4,6\" has three columns. So something is off.\n\nPerhaps the actual data has an additional column for labels, and the totals row adds a total label in the first column while the data rows have empty first column? Not sure.\n\nLet's look at test_column_totals_ignores_text_cells: assert column_totals([[\"a\", 1], [\"b\", 2]]) == [\"\", 3]. Here rows are [[\"a\", 1], [\"b\", 2]]. The first column has text \"a\", \"b\". column_totals returns [\"\", 3]. It returns an empty string for the first column total, and 3 for the second column total. That suggests that when computing totals, non-numeric cells are ignored or treated as empty. So column_totals tries to sum numeric values, and if a cell is not numeric, it skips?
